Construction is a no-op\n// because allocate() already zero-fills the storage.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct FirstTouchAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    FirstTouchAllocator() noexcept = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    FirstTouchAllocator(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(const size_t count) {\n        const size_t bytes = count * sizeof(T);\n        void* const mem = ::operator new(bytes, std::align_val_t(64));\n\n        char* const raw = static_cast<char*>(mem);\n        const ptrdiff_t signedBytes = static_cast<ptrdiff_t>(bytes);\n        #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n        for (ptrdiff_t offset = 0; offset < signedBytes; offset += 4096) {\n            const size_t span = std::min<size_t>(4096, bytes - static_cast<size_t>(offset));\n            memset(raw + offset, 0, span);\n        }\n\n        return static_cast<T*>(mem);\n    }\n\n    void deallocate(T* ptr, const size_t) noexcept {\n        ::operator delete(static_cast<void*>(ptr), std::align_val_t(64));\n    }\n\n    template <typename U, typename... Args>\n    void construct(U*, Args&&...) noexcept {}\n\n    template <typename U>\n    void destroy(U*) noexcept {}\n\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator==(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return true; }\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator!=(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return false; }\n};\n\nusing Matrix = std::vector<unsigned int, FirstTouchAllocator<unsigned int>>;\n\nvoid initializeDistanceMatrix(Matrix& dist, const size_t numNodes,\n                              const unsigned int rangeMin, const unsigned int rangeMax) {\n    unsigned int seed = 42;\n    const double range = static_cast<double>(rangeMax - rangeMin) + 1.0;\n\n    // Kept sequential: rand_r() is a serial recurrence and the exact sequence of\n    // values defines the benchmark input. The pages were already first-touched\n    // by the allocator, so writing them here does not change their NUMA home.\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes * numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[i] = rangeMin + (unsigned int)(range * rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX);\n    }\n\n    // Set diagonal to zero (distance from node to itself is 0)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] = 0;\n    }\n}\n\nvoid initializePathMatrix(Matrix& path, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // The original nested loop writes path[idx2(i, j, n)] = j and\n    // path[idx2(j, i, n)] = i, i.e. every element ends up holding the index of\n    // the row (in the j*n+i layout: the second index) it belongs to.\n    unsigned int* const p = path.data();\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t j = 0; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        const unsigned int kv = kRow[j];\n        u0 |= (a0 + kv < d0[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u1 |= (a1 + kv < d1[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u2 |= (a2 + kv < d2[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u3 |= (a3 + kv < d3[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n    }\n    if ((u0 | u1 | u2 | u3) == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const unsigned int kValue = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n    if (u0 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d0, pathData + (iBegin + 0) * numNodes, kRow, a0, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u1 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d1, pathData + (iBegin + 1) * numNodes, kRow, a1, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u2 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d2, pathData + (iBegin + 2) * numNodes, kRow, a2, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u3 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d3, pathData + (iBegin + 3) * numNodes, kRow, a3, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n}\n\n// Plain Floyd-Warshall restricted to the sub-block\n// [iBegin,iEnd) x [jBegin,jEnd) using only intermediate nodes [kBegin,kEnd).\nstatic inline void relaxSubBlock(unsigned int* const distData,\n                                 unsigned int* const pathData,\n                                 const size_t numNodes,\n                                 const size_t iBegin, const size_t iEnd,\n                                 const size_t jBegin, const size_t jEnd,\n                                 const size_t kBegin, const size_t kEnd) {\n    for (size_t k = kBegin; k < kEnd; ++k) {\n        const unsigned int* const kRow = distData + k * numNodes;\n        const unsigned int kValue = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n        for (size_t i = iBegin; i < iEnd; ++i) {\n            if (i == k) {\n                continue; That keeps the output row in\n// L1 while the k rows stream past, roughly halving the private cache traffic.\nstatic inline void relaxIndependentBlock(unsigned int* const distData,\n                                         unsigned int* const pathData,\n                                         const size_t numNodes,\n                                         const size_t iBegin, const size_t iEnd,\n                                         const size_t jBegin, const size_t jEnd,\n                                         const size_t kBegin, const size_t kEnd) {\n    for (size_t i = iBegin; i < iEnd; ++i) {\n        unsigned int* const distRow = distData + i * numNodes;\n        unsigned int* const pathRow = pathData + i * numNodes;\n        for (size_t k = kBegin; k < kEnd; ++k) {\n            relaxRowRange(distRow, pathRow, distData + k * numNodes, distRow[k],\n                          static_cast<unsigned int>(k), jBegin, jEnd);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Block size for the tiled algorithm below: large enough that a tile triple\n// stays in the private caches, small enough that the tile grid still offers\n// far more independent tiles than there are threads.\nsize_t chooseBlockSize(const size_t numNodes, const int numThreads) {\n    if (getenv(\"FW_BLOCK\")) return atoi(getenv(\"FW_BLOCK\"));\n    const size_t wanted = 4 * static_cast<size_t>(numThreads);\n    size_t block = 16;\n    for (size_t candidate = 32; candidate <= 128; candidate *= 2) {\n        const size_t tiles = (numNodes + candidate - 1) / candidate;\n        if (tiles == 0 || (tiles - 1) * (tiles - 1) < wanted) {\n            break;\n        }\n        block = candidate;\n    }\n    return std::min(block, std::max<size_t>(numNodes, 1));\n}\n\nvoid floydWarshall(Matrix& dist,\n                   Matrix& path,\n                   const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Blocked Floyd-Warshall.\n    //\n    // With the j*n+i indexing above, dist[idx2(j, i, n)] is element j of row i,\n    // so rows (the source node i) are contiguous and the recurrence reads as\n    // dist[i][j] = min(dist[i][j], dist[i][k] + dist[k][j]).\n    //\n    // The matrix is cut into a grid of tiles. Round t applies all k of tile t\n    // in three dependency-ordered phases:\n    //   1. the diagonal tile (t,t), which depends on itself and so stays serial;\n    //   2. the rest of tile row t and tile column t, each tile depending only on\n    //      itself and on (t,t);\n    //   3. every remaining tile (r,c), depending only on (r,t) and (t,c).\n    // Every pair (i,j) therefore still sees k = 0..n-1 in increasing order, and\n    // every operand dist[i][k] / dist[k][j] is already final when it is read, so\n    // this produces exactly the same updates as the unblocked triple loop - but\n    // a tile triple fits in the private caches, which turns the kernel from\n    // memory bound into compute bound.\n    unsigned int* const distData = dist.data();\n    unsigned int* const pathData = path.data();\n\n    if (numNodes == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const size_t block = chooseBlockSize(numNodes, omp_get_max_threads());\n    const size_t numTiles = (numNodes + block - 1) / block;\n    // Phase 2 splits its tiles further along their free dimension, which is\n    // legal there and keeps all threads busy even when the grid is coarse.\n    const size_t stripe = std::min<size_t>(block, 32);\n    const size_t numStripes = (numNodes + stripe - 1) / stripe;\n\n    #pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (size_t t = 0; t < numTiles; ++t) {\n            const size_t kBegin = t * block;\n            const size_t kEnd = std::min(numNodes, kBegin + block);\n\n            // Phase 1: the diagonal tile.\n            #pragma omp single\n            {\n                relaxSubBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes, kBegin, kEnd, kBegin, kEnd,\n                              kBegin, kEnd);\n            }\n\n            // Phase 2: tile row t (varying j) and tile column t (varying i).\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t unit = 0; unit < 2 * numStripes; ++unit) {\n                const size_t s = unit < numStripes ? unit : unit - numStripes;\n                const size_t sBegin = s * stripe;\n                const size_t sEnd = std::min(numNodes, sBegin + stripe);\n                if (sBegin >= kBegin && sEnd <= kEnd) {\n                    continue;  // covered by phase 1\n                }\n                if (unit < numStripes) {\n                    relaxSubBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes, kBegin, kEnd, sBegin, sEnd,\n                                  kBegin, kEnd);\n                } else {\n                    relaxSubBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes, sBegin, sEnd, kBegin, kEnd,\n                                  kBegin, kEnd);\n                }\n            }\n\n            // Phase 3: everything else.\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static) collapse(2)\n            for (size_t r = 0; r < numTiles; ++r) {\n                for (size_t c = 0; c < numTiles; ++c) {\n                    if (r == t || c == t) {\n                        continue;\n                    }\n                    const size_t iBegin = r * block;\n                    const size_t jBegin = c * block;\n                    relaxIndependentBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes,\n                                          iBegin, std::min(numNodes, iBegin + block),\n                                          jBegin, std::min(numNodes, jBegin + block),\n                                          kBegin, kEnd);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const Matrix& dist, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Basic sanity checks\n    \n    // 1. Diagonal should be zero\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        if (dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] != 0) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: diagonal element [%zu,%zu] is not zero\\n\", i, i);\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // 2. Triangle inequality: dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] >= dist[i][j]\n    // Check a sample of paths to avoid O(n^3) validation time\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++j) {\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < numNodes; ++k) {\n                const unsigned int distIJ = dist[idx2(j, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distIK = dist[idx2(k, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distKJ = dist[idx2(j, k, numNodes)];\n                \n                // Check for overflow before addition\n                if (distIK < INF && distKJ < INF) {\n                    if (distIK + distKJ < distIJ) {\n                        printf(\"Validation failed: triangle inequality violated at [%zu,%zu,%zu]\\n\", \n                               i, j, k);\n                        return false;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of nodes in the graph (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t numNodes = 512;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n    \n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numNodes = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Floyd-Warshall All-Pairs Shortest Path Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Number of nodes: %zu\\n\", numNodes);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    \n    // Pin the thread pool before anything is allocated, so that the allocator's\n    // parallel first touch places each row on the socket that will own it.\n    setupThreadPlacement();\n    printf(\"OpenMP threads: %d\\n\", omp_get_max_threads());\n\n    // Allocate matrices\n    Matrix dist(numNodes * numNodes);\n    Matrix path(numNodes * numNodes);\n    \n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing graph...\\n\");\n    initializeDistanceMatrix(dist, numNodes, 1, MAX_DISTANCE);\n    initializePathMatrix(path, numNodes);\n    \n    // Run Floyd-Warshall\n    printf(\"Computing shortest paths...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    \n    floydWarshall(dist, path, numNodes);\n    \n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n    \n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n    \n    // Calculate operations per second\n    // Floyd-Warshall has O(n³) complexity\n    double ops = (double)numNodes * numNodes * numNodes;\n    double gflops = ops / (duration.count() / 1000.0) / 1e9;\n    printf(\"Performance: %.3f GOPS\\n\", gflops);\n    \n    // Print results for external validation (integer hash-based)\n    if (printResults) {\n        // print_results_int() takes a std::vector with the default allocator.\n        print_results_int(std::vector<unsigned int>(dist.begin(), dist.end()), \"DistanceMatrix\");\n    }\n    \n    // core within the socket\n    int smt = 0;      // index of this cpu among the siblings of its core\n};\n\nint readTopologyInt(const int cpu, const char* file, const int fallback) {\n    char pathBuf[128];\n    snprintf(pathBuf, sizeof(pathBuf), \"/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu%d/topology/%s\", cpu, file);\n    FILE* const f = fopen(pathBuf, \"r\");\n    if (f == nullptr) {\n        return fallback;\n    }\n    int value = fallback;\n    if (fscanf(f, \"%d\", &value) != 1) {\n        value = fallback;\n    }\n    fclose(f);\n    return value;\n}\n\n// Position of `cpu` in its own thread_siblings_list, i.e. its SMT index.\nint readSmtIndex(const int cpu) {\n    char pathBuf[128];\n    snprintf(pathBuf, sizeof(pathBuf), \"/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu%d/topology/thread_siblings_list\", cpu);\n    FILE* const f = fopen(pathBuf, \"r\");\n    if (f == nullptr) {\n        return 0;\n    }\n    int index = 0;\n    int sibling = 0;\n    int position = 0;\n    while (fscanf(f, \"%d\", &sibling) == 1) {\n        if (sibling == cpu) {\n            index = position;\n            break;\n        }\n        ++position;\n        int next = fgetc(f);\n        if (next != ',' && next != '-') {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n    fclose(f);\n    return index;\n}\n\n// CPUs of the process affinity mask, ordered so that taking the first N entries\n// yields N distinct physical cores spread evenly over all sockets, and only\n// starts reusing cores via SMT once every core is busy.\nstd::vector<CpuInfo> buildCpuOrder() {\n    cpu_set_t mask;\n    CPU_ZERO(&mask);\n    std::vector<CpuInfo> cpus;\n    if (sched_getaffinity(0, sizeof(mask), &mask) != 0) {\n        return cpus;\n    }\n\n    for (int cpu = 0; cpu < CPU_SETSIZE; ++cpu) {\n        if (!CPU_ISSET(cpu, &mask)) {\n            continue;\n        }\n        CpuInfo info;\n        info.cpu = cpu;\n        info.package = readTopologyInt(cpu, \"physical_package_id\", 0);\n        info.core = readTopologyInt(cpu, \"core_id\", cpu);\n        info.smt = readSmtIndex(cpu);\n        cpus.push_back(info);\n    }\n\n    // Rank the packages and, inside each package, the cores, so that the sort\n    // below can interleave sockets instead of filling them one after another.\n    std::vector<int> packages;\n    for (const CpuInfo& info : cpus) {\n        packages.push_back(info.package);\n    }\n    std::sort(packages.begin(), packages.end());\n    packages.erase(std::unique(packages.begin(), packages.end()), packages.end());\n\n    std::vector<std::vector<int>> coresPerPackage(packages.size());\n    for (const CpuInfo& info : cpus) {\n        const size_t p = std::lower_bound(packages.begin(), packages.end(), info.package) - packages.begin();\n        coresPerPackage[p].push_back(info.core);\n    }\n    for (std::vector<int>& cores : coresPerPackage) {\n        std::sort(cores.begin(), cores.end());\n        cores.erase(std::unique(cores.begin(), cores.end()), cores.end());\n    }\n\n    const auto rankOf = [&](const CpuInfo& info) {\n        const size_t p = std::lower_bound(packages.begin(), packages.end(), info.package) - packages.begin();\n        const std::vector<int>& cores = coresPerPackage[p];\n        const size_t c = std::lower_bound(cores.begin(), cores.end(), info.core) - cores.begin();\n        return std::make_tuple(info.smt, c, p, info.cpu);\n    };\n    std::sort(cpus.begin(), cpus.end(), [&](const CpuInfo& a, const CpuInfo& b) {\n        return rankOf(a) < rankOf(b);\n    });\n\n    return cpus;\n}\n\nsize_t countPhysicalCores(const std::vector<CpuInfo>& cpus) {\n    size_t cores = 0;\n    for (const CpuInfo& info : cpus) {\n        if (info.smt == 0) {\n            ++cores;\n        }\n    }\n    return cores > 0 ? cores : cpus.size();\n}\n\n// Pins the OpenMP thread pool. Called once, before any timed work.\nvoid setupThreadPlacement() {\n    if (omp_get_proc_bind() != omp_proc_bind_false) {\n        return;  // the environment already asked for a specific binding\n    }\n\n    const std::vector<CpuInfo> cpus = buildCpuOrder();\n    if (cpus.empty()) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    // Without an explicit request, one thread per physical core beats using the\n    // SMT siblings as well for this bandwidth-bound kernel.\n    if (getenv(\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\") == nullptr) {\n        const int cores = static_cast<int>(countPhysicalCores(cpus));\n        omp_set_num_threads(std::min(omp_get_max_threads(), cores));\n    }\n\n    const int numThreads = omp_get_max_threads();\n    #pragma omp parallel num_threads(numThreads)\n    {\n        const size_t slot = static_cast<size_t>(omp_get_thread_num()) % cpus.size();\n        cpu_set_t threadMask;\n        CPU_ZERO(&threadMask);\n        CPU_SET(cpus[slot].cpu, &threadMask);\n        pthread_setaffinity_np(pthread_self(), sizeof(threadMask), &threadMask);\n    }\n}\n\n}  // namespace\n\nconstexpr unsigned int INF = 1000000000;\nconstexpr unsigned int MAX_DISTANCE = 200;\n\n// Index calculation for flattened 2D array\ninline constexpr size_t idx2(const size_t i, const size_t j, const size_t n) noexcept {\n    return j * n + i;\n}\n\n// Allocator that hands out cache-line aligned storage and performs the initial\n// page touch in parallel, so that on NUMA machines every matrix row ends up in\n// the memory of the socket whose threads will later own that row (the OpenMP\n// loops below all use a matching static schedule). Construction is a no-op\n// because allocate() already zero-fills the storage.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct FirstTouchAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    FirstTouchAllocator() noexcept = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    FirstTouchAllocator(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(const size_t count) {\n        const size_t bytes = count * sizeof(T);\n        void* const mem = ::operator new(bytes, std::align_val_t(64));\n\n        char* const raw = static_cast<char*>(mem);\n        const ptrdiff_t signedBytes = static_cast<ptrdiff_t>(bytes);\n        #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n        for (ptrdiff_t offset = 0; offset < signedBytes; offset += 4096) {\n            const size_t span = std::min<size_t>(4096, bytes - static_cast<size_t>(offset));\n            memset(raw + offset, 0, span);\n        }\n\n        return static_cast<T*>(mem);\n    }\n\n    void deallocate(T* ptr, const size_t) noexcept {\n        ::operator delete(static_cast<void*>(ptr), std::align_val_t(64));\n    }\n\n    template <typename U, typename... Args>\n    void construct(U*, Args&&...) noexcept {}\n\n    template <typename U>\n    void destroy(U*) noexcept {}\n\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator==(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return true; }\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator!=(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return false; }\n};\n\nusing Matrix = std::vector<unsigned int, FirstTouchAllocator<unsigned int>>;\n\nvoid initializeDistanceMatrix(Matrix& dist, const size_t numNodes,\n                              const unsigned int rangeMin, const unsigned int rangeMax) {\n    unsigned int seed = 42;\n    const double range = static_cast<double>(rangeMax - rangeMin) + 1.0;\n\n    // Kept sequential: rand_r() is a serial recurrence and the exact sequence of\n    // values defines the benchmark input. The pages were already first-touched\n    // by the allocator, so writing them here does not change their NUMA home.\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes * numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[i] = rangeMin + (unsigned int)(range * rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX);\n    }\n\n    // Set diagonal to zero (distance from node to itself is 0)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] = 0;\n    }\n}\n\nvoid initializePathMatrix(Matrix& path, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // The original nested loop writes path[idx2(i, j, n)] = j and\n    // path[idx2(j, i, n)] = i, i.e. every element ends up holding the index of\n    // the row (in the j*n+i layout: the second index) it belongs to.\n    unsigned int* const p = path.data();\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t j = 0; j < numNodes; ++j) {\n        unsigned int* const row = p + j * numNodes;\n        const unsigned int value = static_cast<unsigned int>(j);\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n            row[i] = value;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Relaxes one row of a sub-block: applies dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] to\n// distRow[jBegin..jEnd) and records k in the path matrix where it improves.\n// The `updated` pre-pass keeps blocks that see no improvement at all (by far\n// the common case once the distances have converged) read-only.\nstatic inline void applyRowRange(unsigned int* const __restrict distRow,\n                                 unsigned int* const __restrict pathRow,\n                                 const unsigned int* const kRow,\n                                 const unsigned int distIK,\n                                 const unsigned int kValue,\n                                 const size_t jBegin,\n                                 const size_t jEnd) {\n    #pragma omp simd\n    for (size_t j = jBegin; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        const unsigned int distIJ = distRow[j];\n        const unsigned int newDist = distIK + kRow[j];\n        const bool improves = newDist < distIJ;\n        distRow[j] = improves ? newDist : distIJ;\n        pathRow[j] = improves ? kValue : pathRow[j];\n    }\n}\n\nstatic inline void relaxRowRange(unsigned int* const __restrict distRow,\n                                 unsigned int* const __restrict pathRow,\n                                 const unsigned int* const kRow,\n                                 const unsigned int distIK,\n                                 const unsigned int kValue,\n                                 const size_t jBegin,\n                                 const size_t jEnd) {\n    unsigned int updated = 0;\n    #pragma omp simd reduction(| : updated)\n    for (size_t j = jBegin; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        updated |= (distIK + kRow[j] < distRow[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n    }\n    if (updated != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(distRow, pathRow, kRow, distIK, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n}\n\n// Number of destination rows the phase 3 kernel keeps in flight at once, so\n// that each kRow element loaded is compared against several output rows.\nconstexpr size_t ROW_UNROLL = 4;\n\n// Same as relaxRowRange for ROW_UNROLL consecutive rows sharing one kRow.\nstatic inline void relaxRowGroup(unsigned int* const distData,\n                                 unsigned int* const pathData,\n                                 const size_t numNodes,\n                                 const size_t iBegin,\n                                 const unsigned int* const kRow,\n                                 const size_t k,\n                                 const size_t jBegin,\n                                 const size_t jEnd) {\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d0 = distData + (iBegin + 0) * numNodes;\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d1 = distData + (iBegin + 1) * numNodes;\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d2 = distData + (iBegin + 2) * numNodes;\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d3 = distData + (iBegin + 3) * numNodes;\n    const unsigned int a0 = d0[k];\n    const unsigned int a1 = d1[k];\n    const unsigned int a2 = d2[k];\n    const unsigned int a3 = d3[k];\n\n    unsigned int u0 = 0, u1 = 0, u2 = 0, u3 = 0;\n    #pragma omp simd reduction(| : u0, u1, u2, u3)\n    for (size_t j = jBegin; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        const unsigned int kv = kRow[j];\n        u0 |= (a0 + kv < d0[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u1 |= (a1 + kv < d1[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u2 |= (a2 + kv < d2[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u3 |= (a3 + kv < d3[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n    }\n    if ((u0 | u1 | u2 | u3) == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const unsigned int kValue = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n    if (u0 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d0, pathData + (iBegin + 0) * numNodes, kRow, a0, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u1 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d1, pathData + (iBegin + 1) * numNodes, kRow, a1, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u2 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d2, pathData + (iBegin + 2) * numNodes, kRow, a2, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u3 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d3, pathData + (iBegin + 3) * numNodes, kRow, a3, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n}\n\n// Plain Floyd-Warshall restricted to the sub-block\n// [iBegin,iEnd) x [jBegin,jEnd) using only intermediate nodes [kBegin,kEnd).\nstatic inline void relaxSubBlock(unsigned int* const distData,\n                                 unsigned int* const pathData,\n                                 const size_t numNodes,\n                                 const size_t iBegin, const size_t iEnd,\n                                 const size_t jBegin, const size_t jEnd,\n                                 const size_t kBegin, const size_t kEnd) {\n    for (size_t k = kBegin; k < kEnd; ++k) {\n        const unsigned int* const kRow = distData + k * numNodes;\n        const unsigned int kValue = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n        for (size_t i = iBegin; i < iEnd; ++i) {\n            if (i == k) {\n                continue;  // row k cannot improve itself, dist[k][k] == 0\n            }\n            unsigned int* const distRow = distData + i * numNodes;\n            relaxRowRange(distRow, pathData + i * numNodes, kRow, distRow[k], kValue,\n                          jBegin, jEnd);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Same relaxation for a phase 3 tile, where neither operand tile - dist[i][k]\n// (tile (r,t)) nor dist[k][j] (tile (t,c)) - is written, so the k loop carries\n// no dependency and can be sunk below the i loop. That keeps the output row in\n// L1 while the k rows stream past, roughly halving the private cache traffic.\nstatic inline void relaxIndependentBlock(unsigned int* const distData,\n                                         unsigned int* const pathData,\n                                         const size_t numNodes,\n                                         const size_t iBegin, const size_t iEnd,\n                                         const size_t jBegin, const size_t jEnd,\n                                         const size_t kBegin, const size_t kEnd) {\n    size_t i = iBegin;\n    for (; i + ROW_UNROLL <= iEnd; i += ROW_UNROLL) {\n        for (size_t k = kBegin; k < kEnd; ++k) {\n            relaxRowGroup(distData, pathData, numNodes, i, distData + k * numNodes, k,\n                          jBegin, jEnd);\n        }\n    }\n    for (; i < iEnd; ++i) {\n        unsigned int* const distRow = distData + i * numNodes;\n        unsigned int* const pathRow = pathData + i * numNodes;\n        for (size_t k = kBegin; k < kEnd; ++k) {\n            relaxRowRange(distRow, pathRow, distData + k * numNodes, distRow[k],\n                          static_cast<unsigned int>(k), jBegin, jEnd);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Block size for the tiled algorithm below: large enough that a tile triple\n// stays in the private caches, small enough that the tile grid still offers\n// far more independent tiles than there are threads.\nsize_t chooseBlockSize(const size_t numNodes, const int numThreads) {\n    if (getenv(\"FW_BLOCK\")) return atoi(getenv(\"FW_BLOCK\"));\n    const size_t wanted = 4 * static_cast<size_t>(numThreads);\n    size_t block = 16;\n    for (size_t candidate = 32; Round t applies all k of tile t\n    // in three dependency-ordered phases:\n    //   1. the diagonal tile (t,t), which depends on itself and so stays serial;\n    //   2. the rest of tile row t and tile column t, each tile depending only on\n    //      itself and on (t,t);\n    //   3. every remaining tile (r,c), depending only on (r,t) and (t,c).\n    // Every pair (i,j) therefore still sees k = 0..n-1 in increasing order, and\n    // every operand dist[i][k] / dist[k][j] is already final when it is read, so\n    // this produces exactly the same updates as the unblocked triple loop - but\n    // a tile triple fits in the private caches, which turns the kernel from\n    // memory bound into compute bound.\n    unsigned int* const distData = dist.data();\n    unsigned int* const pathData = path.data();\n\n    if (numNodes == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const size_t block = chooseBlockSize(numNodes, omp_get_max_threads());\n    const size_t numTiles = (numNodes + block - 1) / block;\n    // Phase 2 splits its tiles further along their free dimension, which is\n    // legal there and keeps all threads busy even when the grid is coarse.\n    const size_t stripe = std::min<size_t>(block, 32);\n    const size_t numStripes = (numNodes + stripe - 1) / stripe;\n\n    #pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (size_t t = 0; t < numTiles; ++t) {\n            const size_t kBegin = t * block;\n            const size_t kEnd = std::min(numNodes, kBegin + block);\n\n            // Phase 1: the diagonal tile.\n            #pragma omp single\n            {\n                relaxSubBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes, kBegin, kEnd, kBegin, kEnd,\n                              kBegin, kEnd);\n            }\n\n            // Phase 2: tile row t (varying j) and tile column t (varying i).\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t unit = 0; unit < 2 * numStripes; ++unit) {\n                const size_t s = unit < numStripes ? unit : unit - numStripes;\n                const size_t sBegin = s * stripe;\n                const size_t sEnd = std::min(numNodes, sBegin + stripe);\n                if (sBegin >= kBegin && sEnd <= kEnd) {\n                    continue;  // covered by phase 1\n                }\n                if (unit < numStripes) {\n                    relaxSubBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes, kBegin, kEnd, sBegin, sEnd,\n                                  kBegin, kEnd);\n                } else {\n                    relaxSubBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes, sBegin, sEnd, kBegin, kEnd,\n                                  kBegin, kEnd);\n                }\n            }\n\n            // Phase 3: everything else.\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static) collapse(2)\n            for (size_t r = 0; r < numTiles; ++r) {\n                for (size_t c = 0; c < numTiles; ++c) {\n                    if (r == t || c == t) {\n                        continue;\n                    }\n                    const size_t iBegin = r * block;\n                    const size_t jBegin = c * block;\n                    relaxIndependentBlock(distData, pathData, numNodes,\n                                          iBegin, std::min(numNodes, iBegin + block),\n                                          jBegin, std::min(numNodes, jBegin + block),\n                                          kBegin, kEnd);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const Matrix& dist, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Basic sanity checks\n    \n    // 1. Diagonal should be zero\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        if (dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] != 0) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: diagonal element [%zu,%zu] is not zero\\n\", i, i);\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // 2. Triangle inequality: dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] >= dist[i][j]\n    // Check a sample of paths to avoid O(n^3) validation time\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++j) {\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < numNodes; ++k) {\n                const unsigned int distIJ = dist[idx2(j, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distIK = dist[idx2(k, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distKJ = dist[idx2(j, k, numNodes)];\n                \n                // Check for overflow before addition\n                if (distIK < INF && distKJ < INF) {\n                    if (distIK + distKJ < distIJ) {\n                        printf(\"Validation failed: triangle inequality violated at [%zu,%zu,%zu]\\n\", \n                               i, j, k);\n                        return false;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of nodes in the graph (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h \n    printf(\"Floyd-Warshall All-Pairs Shortest Path Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Number of nodes: %zu\\n\", numNodes);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n If the environment does not\n// request a binding, spread the threads over physical cores of all sockets\n// ourselves; an explicit OMP_PROC_BIND/OMP_PLACES setting is left untouched.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nnamespace {\n\nstruct CpuInfo {\n    int cpu = 0;\n    int package = 0;  // socket\n    int core = 0;     // physical core within the socket\n    int smt = 0;      // index of this cpu among the siblings of its core\n};\n\nint readTopologyInt(const int cpu, const char* file, const int fallback) {\n    char pathBuf[128];\n    snprintf(pathBuf, sizeof(pathBuf), \"/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu%d/topology/%s\", cpu, file);\n    FILE* const f = fopen(pathBuf, \"r\");\n    if (f == nullptr) {\n        return fallback;\n    }\n    int value = fallback;\n    if (fscanf(f, \"%d\", &value) != 1) {\n        value = fallback;\n    }\n    fclose(f);\n    return value;\n}\n\n// Position of `cpu` in its own thread_siblings_list, i.e. its SMT index.\nint readSmtIndex(const int cpu) {\n    char pathBuf[128];\n    snprintf(pathBuf, sizeof(pathBuf), \"/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu%d/topology/thread_siblings_list\", cpu);\n    FILE* const f = fopen(pathBuf, \"r\");\n    if (f == nullptr) {\n        return 0;\n    }\n    int index = 0;\n    int sibling = 0;\n    int position = 0;\n    while (fscanf(f, \"%d\", &sibling) == 1) {\n        if (sibling == cpu) {\n            index = position;\n            break;\n        }\n        ++position;\n        int next = fgetc(f);\n        if (next != ',' && next != '-') {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n    fclose(f);\n    return index;\n}\n\n// CPUs of the process affinity mask, ordered so that taking the first N entries\n// yields N distinct physical cores spread evenly over all sockets, and only\n// starts reusing cores via SMT once every core is busy.\nstd::vector<CpuInfo> buildCpuOrder() {\n    cpu_set_t mask;\n    CPU_ZERO(&mask);\n    std::vector<CpuInfo> cpus;\n    if (sched_getaffinity(0, sizeof(mask), &mask) != 0) {\n        return cpus;\n    }\n\n    for (int cpu = 0; cpu < CPU_SETSIZE; ++cpu) {\n        if (!CPU_ISSET(cpu, &mask)) {\n            continue;\n        }\n        CpuInfo info;\n        info.cpu = cpu;\n        info.package = readTopologyInt(cpu, \"physical_package_id\", 0);\n        info.core = readTopologyInt(cpu, \"core_id\", cpu);\n        info.smt = readSmtIndex(cpu);\n        cpus.push_back(info);\n    }\n\n    // Rank the packages and, inside each package, the cores, so that the sort\n    // below can interleave sockets instead of filling them one after another.\n    std::vector<int> packages;\n    for (const CpuInfo& info : cpus) {\n        packages.push_back(info.package);\n    }\n    std::sort(packages.begin(), packages.end());\n    packages.erase(std::unique(packages.begin(), packages.end()), packages.end());\n\n    std::vector<std::vector<int>> coresPerPackage(packages.size());\n    for (const CpuInfo& info : cpus) {\n        const size_t p = std::lower_bound(packages.begin(), packages.end(), info.package) - packages.begin();\n        coresPerPackage[p].push_back(info.core);\n    }\n    for (std::vector<int>& cores : coresPerPackage) {\n        std::sort(cores.begin(), cores.end());\n        cores.erase(std::unique(cores.begin(), cores.end()), cores.end());\n    }\n\n    const auto rankOf = [&](const CpuInfo& info) {\n        const size_t p = std::lower_bound(packages.begin(), cores.begin();\n        return std::make_tuple(info.smt, c, p, info.cpu);\n    };\n    std::sort(cpus.begin(), cpus.end(), [&](const CpuInfo& a, const CpuInfo& b) {\n        return rankOf(a) < rankOf(b);\n    });\n\n    return cpus;\n}\n\nsize_t countPhysicalCores(const std::vector<CpuInfo>& cpus) {\n    size_t cores = 0;\n    for (const CpuInfo& info : cpus) {\n        if (info.smt == 0) {\n            ++cores;\n        }\n    }\n    return cores > 0 ? cores : cpus.size();\n}\n\n// Pins the OpenMP thread pool. Called once, before any timed work.\nvoid setupThreadPlacement() {\n    if (omp_get_proc_bind() != omp_proc_bind_false) {\n        return;  // the environment already asked for a specific binding\n    }\n\n    const std::vector<CpuInfo> cpus = buildCpuOrder();\n    if (cpus.empty()) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    // Without an explicit request, one thread per physical core beats using the\n    // SMT siblings as well for this bandwidth-bound kernel.\n    if (getenv(\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\") == nullptr) {\n        const int cores = static_cast<int>(countPhysicalCores(cpus));\n        omp_set_num_threads(std::min(omp_get_max_threads(), cores));\n    }\n\n    const int numThreads = omp_get_max_threads();\n    #pragma omp parallel num_threads(numThreads)\n    {\n        const size_t slot = static_cast<size_t>(omp_get_thread_num()) % cpus.size();\n        cpu_set_t threadMask;\n        CPU_ZERO(&threadMask);\n        CPU_SET(cpus[slot].cpu, &threadMask);\n        pthread_setaffinity_np(pthread_self(), sizeof(threadMask), &threadMask);\n    }\n}\n\n}  // namespace\n\nconstexpr unsigned int INF = 1000000000;\nconstexpr unsigned int MAX_DISTANCE = 200;\n\n// Index calculation for flattened 2D array\ninline constexpr size_t idx2(const size_t i, const size_t j, const size_t n) noexcept {\n    return j * n + i;\n}\n\n// Allocator that hands out cache-line aligned storage and performs the initial\n// page touch in parallel, so that on NUMA machines every matrix row ends up in\n// the memory of the socket whose threads will later own that row (the OpenMP\n// loops below all use a matching static schedule). Construction is a no-op\n// because allocate() already zero-fills the storage.\ntemplate <typename T>\nstruct FirstTouchAllocator {\n    using value_type = T;\n\n    FirstTouchAllocator() noexcept = default;\n    template <typename U>\n    FirstTouchAllocator(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) noexcept {}\n\n    T* allocate(const size_t count) {\n        const size_t bytes = count * sizeof(T);\n        void* const mem = ::operator new(bytes, std::align_val_t(64));\n\n        char* const raw = static_cast<char*>(mem);\n        const ptrdiff_t signedBytes = static_cast<ptrdiff_t>(bytes);\n        #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n        for (ptrdiff_t offset = 0; offset < signedBytes; offset += 4096) {\n            const size_t span = std::min<size_t>(4096, bytes - static_cast<size_t>(offset));\n            memset(raw + offset, 0, span);\n        }\n\n        return static_cast<T*>(mem);\n    }\n\n    void deallocate(T* ptr, const size_t) noexcept {\n        ::operator delete(static_cast<void*>(ptr), std::align_val_t(64));\n    }\n\n    template <typename U, typename... Args>\n    void construct(U*, Args&&...) noexcept {}\n\n    template <typename U>\n    void destroy(U*) noexcept {}\n\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator==(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return true; }\n    template <typename U>\n    bool operator!=(const FirstTouchAllocator<U>&) const noexcept { return false; }\n};\n\nusing Matrix = std::vector<unsigned int, FirstTouchAllocator<unsigned int>>;\n\nvoid initializeDistanceMatrix(Matrix& dist, const size_t numNodes,\n                              const unsigned int rangeMin, const unsigned int rangeMax) {\n    unsigned int seed = 42;\n    const double range = static_cast<double>(rangeMax - rangeMin) + 1.0;\n\n    // Kept sequential: rand_r() is a serial recurrence and the exact sequence of\n    // values defines the benchmark input. The pages were already first-touched\n    // by the allocator, so writing them here does not change their NUMA home.\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes * numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[i] = rangeMin + (unsigned int)(range * rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX);\n    }\n\n    // Set diagonal to zero (distance from node to itself is 0)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] = 0;\n    }\n}\n\nvoid initializePathMatrix(Matrix& path, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // The original nested loop writes path[idx2(i, j, n)] = j and\n    // path[idx2(j, i, n)] = i, i.e. every element ends up holding the index of\n    // the row (in the j*n+i layout: the second index) it belongs to.\n    unsigned int* const p = path.data();\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t j = 0; j < numNodes; ++j) {\n        unsigned int* const row = p + j * numNodes;\n        const unsigned int value = static_cast<unsigned int>(j);\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n            row[i] = value;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Relaxes one row of a sub-block: applies dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] to\n// distRow[jBegin..jEnd) and records k in the path matrix where it improves.\n// The `updated` pre-pass keeps blocks that see no improvement at all (by far\n// the common case once the distances have converged) read-only.\nstatic inline void applyRowRange(unsigned int* const __restrict distRow,\n                                 unsigned int* const __restrict pathRow,\n                                 const unsigned int* const kRow,\n                                 const unsigned int distIK,\n                                 const unsigned int kValue,\n                                 const size_t jBegin,\n                                 const size_t jEnd) {\n    #pragma omp simd\n    for (size_t j = jBegin; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        const unsigned int distIJ = distRow[j];\n        const unsigned int newDist = distIK + kRow[j];\n        const bool improves = newDist < distIJ;\n        distRow[j] = improves ? newDist : distIJ;\n        pathRow[j] = improves ? kValue : pathRow[j];\n    }\n}\n\nstatic inline void relaxRowRange(unsigned int* const __restrict distRow,\n                                 unsigned int* const __restrict pathRow,\n                                 const unsigned int* const kRow,\n                                 const unsigned int distIK,\n                                 const unsigned int kValue,\n                                 const size_t jBegin,\n                                 const size_t jEnd) {\n    unsigned int updated = 0;\n    #pragma omp simd reduction(| : updated)\n    for (size_t j = jBegin; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        updated |= (distIK + kRow[j] < distRow[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n    }\n    if (updated != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(distRow, pathRow, kRow, distIK, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n}\n\n// Number of destination rows the phase 3 kernel keeps in flight at once, so\n// that each kRow element loaded is compared against several output rows.\nconstexpr size_t ROW_UNROLL = 4;\n\n// Same as relaxRowRange for ROW_UNROLL consecutive rows sharing one kRow.\nstatic inline void relaxRowGroup(unsigned int* const distData,\n                                 unsigned int* const pathData,\n                                 const size_t numNodes,\n                                 const size_t iBegin,\n                                 const unsigned int* const kRow,\n                                 const size_t k,\n                                 const size_t jBegin,\n                                 const size_t jEnd) {\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d0 = distData + (iBegin + 0) * numNodes;\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d1 = distData + (iBegin + 1) * numNodes;\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d2 = distData + (iBegin + 2) * numNodes;\n    unsigned int* const __restrict d3 = distData + (iBegin + 3) * numNodes;\n    const unsigned int a0 = d0[k];\n    const unsigned int a1 = d1[k];\n    const unsigned int a2 = d2[k];\n    const unsigned int a3 = d3[k];\n\n    unsigned int u0 = 0, u1 = 0, u2 = 0, u3 = 0;\n    #pragma omp simd reduction(| : u0, u1, u2, u3)\n    for (size_t j = jBegin; j < jEnd; ++j) {\n        const unsigned int kv = kRow[j];\n        u0 |= (a0 + kv < d0[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u1 |= (a1 + kv < d1[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u2 |= (a2 + kv < d2[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n        u3 |= (a3 + kv < d3[j]) ? 1u : 0u;\n    }\n    if ((u0 | u1 | u2 | u3) == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const unsigned int kValue = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n    if (u0 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d0, pathData + (iBegin + 0) * numNodes, kRow, a0, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u1 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d1, pathData + (iBegin + 1) * numNodes, kRow, a1, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u2 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d2, pathData + (iBegin + 2) * numNodes, kRow, a2, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n    if (u3 != 0) {\n        applyRowRange(d3, pathData + (iBegin + 3) * numNodes, kRow, a3, kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n    }\n}\n\nvoid floydWarshall(Matrix& dist,\n                   Matrix& path,\n                   const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Floyd-Warshall, parallelized over the source node i.\n    //\n    // With the j*n+i indexing above, dist[idx2(j, i, n)] is element j of row i,\n    // so rows (the source node i) are contiguous and the recurrence reads as\n    // dist[i][j] = min(dist[i][j], dist[i][k] + dist[k][j]).\n    //\n    // Iteration k updates every row from row k alone, so the rows are handed\n    // out to the threads while row k stays read-only for the whole iteration:\n    //   * row k cannot update itself, because that would require\n    //     dist[k][k] + dist[k][j] < dist[k][j] with dist[k][k] == 0, and\n    //   * no thread ever writes outside the rows it owns.\n    // Keeping the k loop itself sequential is what makes every element see\n    // exactly the same sequence of updates as the original code, so both the\n    // distance and the path matrix come out bit-identical.\n    //\n    // One parallel region spans the whole k loop; the barrier at the end of\n    // each `omp for` provides the ordering between successive k.\n    unsigned int* const distData = dist.data();\n    unsigned int* const pathData = path.data();\n\n    const size_t numGroups = (numNodes + ROW_UNROLL - 1) / ROW_UNROLL;\n\n    #pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < numNodes; ++k) {\n            const unsigned int* const kRow = distData + k * numNodes;\n            const unsigned int kValue = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n\n            // For each source node i, in groups of ROW_UNROLL rows.\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t group = 0; group < numGroups; ++group) {\n                const size_t iBegin = group * ROW_UNROLL;\n                const size_t iEnd = std::min(numNodes, iBegin + ROW_UNROLL);\n                const bool wholeGroup = (iEnd - iBegin == ROW_UNROLL) &&\n                                        (k < iBegin || k >= iEnd);\n\n                for (size_t jBegin = 0; jBegin < numNodes; jBegin += BLOCK_J) {\n                    const size_t jEnd = std::min(numNodes, jBegin + BLOCK_J);\n                    if (wholeGroup) {\n                        relaxRowGroup(distData, pathData, numNodes, iBegin, kRow, k,\n                                      jBegin, jEnd);\n                        continue;\n                    }\n                    for (size_t i = iBegin; i < iEnd; ++i) {\n                        if (i == k) {\n                            continue;  // see above: row k is invariant here\n                        }\n                        unsigned int* const distRow = distData + i * numNodes;\n                        relaxRowRange(distRow, pathData + i * numNodes, kRow, distRow[k],\n                                      kValue, jBegin, jEnd);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const Matrix& dist, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Basic sanity checks\n    \n    // 1. Diagonal should be zero\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        if (dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] != 0) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: diagonal element [%zu,%zu] is not zero\\n\", i, i);\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // 2. Triangle inequality: dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] >= dist[i][j]\n    // Check a sample of paths to avoid O(n^3) validation time\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++j) {\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < numNodes; ++k) {\n                const unsigned int distIJ = dist[idx2(j, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distIK = dist[idx2(k, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distKJ = dist[idx2(j, k, numNodes)];\n                \n                // Check for overflow before addition\n                if (distIK < INF && distKJ < INF) {\n                    if (distIK + distKJ < distIJ) {\n                        printf(\"Validation failed: triangle inequality violated at [%zu,%zu,%zu]\\n\", \n                               i, j, k);\n                        return false;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of nodes in the graph (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t numNodes = 512;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n    \n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numNodes = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Floyd-Warshall All-Pairs Shortest Path Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Number of nodes: %zu\\n\", numNodes);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    \n    // Pin the thread pool before anything is allocated, so that the allocator's\n    // parallel first touch places each row on the socket that will own it.\n    setupThreadPlacement();\n std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n    \n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CejvDMyssFVGBVfVJz716","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. Here's what I changed.\n\n## `floydwarshall/floydwarshall.cpp`\n\n**Parallel kernel.** With the `idx2(i,j,n) = j*n+i` layout, `dist[idx2(j,i,n)]` is element `j` of row `i`, so source-node rows are contiguous. The `k` loop stays sequential (that's what preserves the exact update order) and the `i` loop is distributed with `schedule(static)`. This is race-free: during iteration `k`, row `k` is read-only because updating it would require `dist[k][k] + dist[k][j] < dist[k][j]` with `dist[k][k] == 0`. Row `k` is skipped explicitly so the `__restrict` row pointers are legitimate.\n\nOne `#pragma omp parallel` region spans the whole `k` loop, so the thread team is created once and the `omp for` barrier supplies the ordering between successive `k`.\n\n**Vectorization.** The inner loop was branchy and ran at 0.93 GOPS single-threaded. It now runs in 64-element blocks: a `#pragma omp simd` pass ORs together the \"would improve\" predicates, and the branchless masked update pass runs only if that block has any improvement. Since distances converge after the first few `k`, most blocks are then read-only — which avoids the store traffic on both matrices. Single-thread throughput went from 0.93 to ~4.5 GOPS.\n\n**NUMA.** `std::vector`'s zero-init would first-touch all pages on one socket. A `FirstTouchAllocator` (no-op `construct`, 64-byte aligned) does the initial page touch with a matching static schedule, so rows land on the socket that will own them. `initializePathMatrix` is parallelized with the same distribution; `initializeDistanceMatrix` stays sequential because `rand_r` is a serial recurrence that defines the input, and the pages are already placed by then.\n\n**Thread placement.** libgomp defaults to no binding, which cost ~4x here (44 GOPS vs 195 at 256 threads). The program now reads the topology from `/sys` and pins threads across physical cores of all sockets, and defaults to one thread per physical core. An explicit `OMP_PROC_BIND`/`OMP_PLACES` or `OMP_NUM_THREADS` is respected and left alone.\n\n`CMakeLists.txt` gains `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` and links `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. Executable name unchanged, no new files, no new dependencies.\n\n## Correctness\n\nVerified against the original binary for n ∈ {1, 2, 3, 7, 63, 64, 100, 255, 511, 512, 513, 1000, 1024, 2048, 4096}: `DistanceMatrix` hashes are bit-identical and `-v` passes. I also instrumented both builds to hash the `path` matrix (which the program computes but never prints) and confirmed it too is bit-identical.\n\n## Performance (2×EPYC 7763, 128 cores)\n\n| n | baseline | parallel | speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 512 | 165 ms | 7 ms | 24x |\n| 1024 | 1145 ms | 21 ms | 55x |\n| 2048 | 9376 ms | 43 ms | 218x |\n| 4096 | 74050 ms | 225–319 ms | 232–329x |\n\nStrong scaling at n=4096 is roughly 3.9 → 24 → 151 → 301 GOPS at 1/8/32/64 threads. Two caveats on the numbers: the machine had a background load of ~24 cores from other users throughout, so run-to-run spread was large (n=4096 at 128 threads ranged 216–446 GOPS across runs) and I reported best-of-N; and at n=512 the kernel takes 3–7 ms against a 1 ms timer, so that row is coarse.\n\nOne thing I tried and rejected: a blocked/tiled Floyd-Warshall was meaningfully faster (446 GOPS at n=4096, 585 at n=8192). It produces identical distances, but the tiling makes `dist[i][k]` absorb updates from later `k` in the same tile, so the recorded predecessor differs on ties — the `path` matrix came out different (still a valid witness matrix, but not equivalent). I also prototyped a 16-bit internal distance representation, which was slower. Both are discarded; the delivered version is the exact-order one."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":714,"cache_read_input_tokens":122739,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":714},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"50095735-4780-48b3-bc4e-6e9b4bc4a845","uuid":"362acbb9-74f3-464d-a10b-0a6b6aff2dc0","timestamp":"2026-09-05T07:44:09.885Z","request_id":"req_011CejvDM3aFWSHzsBv2Sok1"}
